<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> For every fixed integer <i>s</i> ≥ 6, we determine the sharp logarithmic exponent of the off-diagonal Ramsey number:
> 
> $`\displaystyle r(s,t)=\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{s-2+o(1)}} \qquad (t\longrightarrow\infty).`$

- **C01** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-main)): For every fixed integer $s\ge6$ there is a constant $C_s>0$ such that, for every $\varepsilon>0$ and every sufficiently large integer $t$, $$\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{s-2+\varepsilon}} \le r(s,t) \le C_s\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{s-2}}.$$ In particular, $$\lim_{t\to\infty} \frac{(s-1)\log t-\log r(s,t)}{\log\log t}=s-2.$$
  - Explanation: Determines the logarithmic exponent for fixed clique order, with an epsilon-slack lower bound and a constant-prefactor upper bound; it does not supply a matching constant-prefactor lower bound.
- **C02** (theorem, [source](#stmt-intro-prime-construction)): Fix an integer $d\ge5$ and a real number $0<\eta<1/10$. For every sufficiently large prime $q$, with $\sigma=\log q$, there is a graph $G$ such that $$\|V(G)\|=\lfloor q^d\sigma\rfloor,\qquad K_{d+1}\not\subseteq G,\qquad \alpha(G)<\lfloor q\sigma^{1+\eta}\rfloor.$$
  - Explanation: Existential prime-indexed flag graph construction with the stated vertex count, clique exclusion and independence-number bound; no efficient search time is asserted.
- **C03** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-incidence)): Let $j\ge2$, and put $p_j=Q_{j-1}/Q_j=(1-Q_j^{-1})/q$. For any real weights $w_x$ on one of the two projective spaces, $$\sum_y\left(\sum_{x\perp y}w_x-p_j\sum_xw_x\right)^2 =q^{j-1}\left(\sum_xw_x^2-\frac{(\sum_xw_x)^2}{Q_j}\right) \le q^{j-1}\sum_xw_x^2. \label{base:weighted-variance}$$ In particular, for sets $S,T$ in the opposite spaces, $$\left\|e(S,T)-p_j\|S\|\|T\|\right\| \le q^{(j-1)/2}\sqrt{\|S\|\|T\|}. \label{base:mixing}$$ If $S,T$ are nonempty and $e(S,T)/(\|S\|\|T\|)=o(1/q)$, then $\|S\|\|T\|\le Cq^{j+1}$ for all sufficiently large $q$.
  - Explanation: Exact weighted point-hyperplane variance identity yields incidence mixing and the sparse-support product bound.
- **C04** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-flag-graph)): Every graph defined by Equation <a href="#base:edge-rule" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:edge-rule">[base:edge-rule]</a> is $K_{d+1}$-free. Its independent sets, in increasing position order, are exactly its consistent subtuples. Reversing a consistent tuple and interchanging its first and second endpoints preserves consistency.
  - Explanation: Ordered incident-flag edge rule excludes the stated clique and identifies independent ordered subtuples with consistency; dual reversal preserves consistency.
- **C05** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-rectangles)): There is a constant $C_d$ such that, with probability $1-o(1)$, every $\mathcal R(U)$ contains at most $C_d\sigma$ positions of the uniform stream. The same event controls rectangles after dual interchange.
  - Explanation: Simultaneous high-probability occupancy control applies to every subspace rectangle, including adaptive choices.
- **C06** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-selection-entropy)): Let a random stream have law dominated by $C_0$ times the iid uniform flag-stream law, where $C_0$ is independent of $q$. From this stream, using arbitrary dependent auxiliary randomness, extract a tuple $F$ of deterministic length $\ell=\Theta(k)$. Its order may be either increasing stream order or reversed stream order with the endpoints interchanged. Then $$H(F)\ge d\sigma\ell+\eta\ell\log\sigma-O(k). \label{base:entropy-lower}$$ The implicit constant may depend on $C_0$ and the fixed comparison constants in $\ell=\Theta(k)$.
  - Explanation: Arbitrary tuple selection retains an entropy surplus under constant stream-law domination; independence of selected flags is unnecessary.
- **C07** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-event-transfer)): For two probability laws $P,Q$ on the same finite or countable space and an event $E$, $$Q(E)\le\frac{\mathop{\mathrm{KL}}(P\Vert Q)+P(E)}{1-e^{-1}}. \label{base:kl-event}$$
  - Explanation: Transfers event probabilities from a joint law to a comparison law using relative entropy, including the infinite-divergence case.
- **C08** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-tails)): If $X$ is binomial or Poisson with mean $\mu$, then $\mathbb Ee^{tX}\le\exp((e^t-1)\mu)$ for every real $t$. For each fixed $0<\delta<1$ there is $c_\delta>0$ such that $$\mathbb P(\|X-\mu\|\ge\delta\mu)\le2e^{-c_\delta\mu}.$$ For $h\ge e\mu>0$, $$\mathbb P(X\ge h)\le(e\mu/h)^h.$$ When $\mu=0$, the variable is zero almost surely.
  - Explanation: Exponential moments give proportional and upper tails for binomial and Poisson sampling; zero mean is handled separately.
- **C09** (bound, [source](#stmt-base-scale-comparisons)): Uniformly over the interval for $D$ in Equation <a href="#base:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="base:parameters">[base:parameters]</a>, $$K_*=o(L),\qquad L=o(P),\qquad P=o(\sigma),\qquad R\log L+\log\sigma=o(P).$$ Moreover, for sufficiently large $q$, $D\sigma^{9\beta}\le P\le4D\sigma^{9\beta}$.
  - Explanation: The deterministic parameter interval separates the description scales asymptotically.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-description)): Let $2\leq j\leq d$. Let $S,T$ be nonempty sets in opposite point systems of $\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(j,q)$, contained in decoder-known sets $U_S,U_T$. Suppose, for a fixed constant $C_b$, that $$\label{geom:sparse-hypotheses} \|S\|\|T\|\geq q^{j+1}e^{-b},\qquad \frac{e(S,T)}{\|S\|\|T\|}\leq\frac{\tau}{q},\qquad 0\leq b\leq C_bK_*,\quad 0<\tau\leq C_b\sigma^{-100\beta}.$$ Write $$d_S=\log\frac{\|U_S\|}{\|S\|},\qquad d_T=\log\frac{\|U_T\|}{\|T\|},$$ and orient the systems so that $n=\|S\|\leq \|T\|$. There is a public-table description, of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P),$$ which, except with probability $Ce^{-cq}$, produces a decoder-known set $W\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\label{geom:description-output} \|W\|\leq ne^{CP},\qquad \|W\cap S\|\geq cn.$$ The probability bound holds conditional on any earlier data independent of the tables used by this invocation.
  - Explanation: A sparse pair in opposite projective systems has a public-table description capturing a fixed fraction of its smaller support.
- **C11** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-validation)): Under <a href="#geom:sparse-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geom:sparse-hypotheses">[geom:sparse-hypotheses]</a>, suppose a decoder-known $W\subseteq U_S$ satisfies <a href="#geom:description-output" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geom:description-output">[geom:description-output]</a>. Further independent tables and a message of length $CqP(d_S+d_T+1)$ produce caps $U_T'\subseteq U_T$ and $U_S'\subseteq U_S$, with additional failure probability $Ce^{-cq}$, such that $$\label{geom:validation-output} \begin{aligned} \|U_T'\|&\leq \frac{Cq^{j+1}}{\|S\|},& \|U_S'\|&\leq \frac{Cq^{j+1}}{\|T\|},\\ \|U_T'\cap T\|&\geq .99\|T\|,& \|U_S'\cap S\|&\geq .99\|S\|. \end{aligned}$$ Each cap is its input domain intersected with an ambient test: a candidate passes if it is incident with fewer than $h/(5q)$ entries of the transmitted row of length $h$ in the opposite system.
  - Explanation: Two fresh validation tables create caps capturing nearly all original supports, with bounds on both ambient sizes.
- **C12** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-produced-test)): Consider either test in Lemma <a href="#geom:validation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:validation">11</a>, conditional on all data making that test ready, but not on any later event. Suppose its hidden source $X_0$ is a subset of an original support $X$ with $\|X_0\|\geq c\|X\|$. For every fixed opposite point $y$, $$\label{geom:exclusion-bound} \mathbb P\{\text{the test is produced and its ambient test excludes }y\} \leq Cq\frac{\|X\cap N(y)\|}{\|X\|}.$$ Conditional on production, the row law is dominated by a constant times the independent uniform row law on $X_0$. The bound <a href="#geom:exclusion-bound" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geom:exclusion-bound">[geom:exclusion-bound]</a> may be averaged over earlier readiness data whenever its right-hand side is fixed.
  - Explanation: A produced validation row has constant iid-law domination at readiness, with a pointwise exclusion bound; later successes are excluded from conditioning.
- **C13** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-projection)): For $j\geq4$, assume Lemma <a href="#geom:description" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:description">10</a> in projective dimension $j-1$, for every fixed value of its hypothesis constant. Then the lemma holds in dimension $j$.
  - Explanation: Repeated projections and cap intersections lift the lower-dimensional sparse-pair description to higher dimensions.
- **C14** (construction, [source](#stmt-geom-preparation)): Suppose <a href="#geom:sparse-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="geom:sparse-hypotheses">[geom:sparse-hypotheses]</a> holds with $j\in\{2,3\}$ and $n=\|S\|\leq\|T\|$. Either enumeration already proves Lemma <a href="#geom:description" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:description">10</a>, or the following preparation costs $O(q+\sigma)$ bits. In dimension three there is also a possible reduction to the dimension-two description lemma, with the same final cost, size, capture, and failure bounds as Lemma <a href="#geom:description" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:description">10</a>.* *In the case not resolved by enumeration or that reduction, we have $n>100qP$, a retained set $S'\subseteq S$ of size $n'\in[n/2,n]$, and either an empty list or an ordered plane list $\Pi_1,\ldots,\Pi_J$ in $\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(3,q)$. In the latter case the sets $$\label{geom:cells} C_i=(S\cap\Pi_i)\setminus\bigcup_{h<i}\Pi_h \quad(1\leq i\leq J)$$ partition $S'$, have nonincreasing sizes, and satisfy $\|C_i\|<.04n'$. The decoder knows the list and all cell sizes, but need not know $S'$ or the cells themselves.* *Write $$\label{geom:lowdim-parameters} n=q^{1+v},\quad g=(v-1/2)\sigma,\quad \chi=P/100,\quad\chi_0=P/10000,\quad B=q^j/n'.$$ For every ambient query point $x$, define $O_x$ to be the cell $C_i$ of its earliest containing listed plane, and $O_x=\varnothing$ if there is none. Define $$\label{geom:outside-support} f_x=\|O_x\|/n'\leq .04,\qquad X_x=S'\setminus(O_x\cup\{x\}).$$ If no list is present, $O_x=\varnothing$ for all $x$. When $j=3$ and $g>\chi_0$, the preparation additionally ensures either* 1. *every plane meets $S'$ in fewer than $K_p=n^{4/3}q^{-1}e^{-g/5}$ points and the list is empty; or* 2. *the list is present and $J\leq n/K_p=q^{1/2}e^{-2g/15}$.
  - Explanation: Low-dimensional preparation either enumerates a small support, reduces a large planar cell, or prepares a small-cell support.
- **C15** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-rich-lines)): Let $q$ run through sufficiently large primes. Let $\mathcal S$ be at most $n\leq Cq^2$ points of $\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(3,q)$, with at most $K_p$ points in every projective plane. Let $M>0$ satisfy $$\label{geom:rich-regime} \frac{M^3}{n}\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad \frac{M^2}{K_p}\longrightarrow\infty,\qquad cq\leq\frac nM\leq Cq^{41/40}.$$ Then at most $Cn/M$ projective lines contain at least $M$ points of $\mathcal S$. The constant is uniform once the displayed limits hold uniformly in the family under consideration.
  - Explanation: Polynomial interpolation, plane caps and characteristic control bound rich lines in the stated asymptotic regime.
- **C16** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-radial)): Consider the dimension-three output of Proposition <a href="#geom:preparation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:preparation">14</a>. A line $\ell$ through a query point $x$ has strength $$a_\ell(x)=q\|X_x\cap\ell\|/n'.$$ Outside one exceptional set of $o(n)$ ambient points, simultaneously for dyadic $a\in[q^{-10},2]$, $$\label{geom:radial-count} \#\{\ell\ni x:a_\ell(x)\geq a\} \leq q^{2-2v}e^\chi a^{-100}.$$ In addition, whenever $g>\chi_0$ and $a>e^{-g/20}$ with $0<a\leq2$, the total number of ambient center–line pairs $(x,\ell)$ having strength at least $a$ is at most $Cq^2/a^2$.
  - Explanation: Own-cell removal controls radial rich lines except at a negligible set of centers, with a separate center-line bound.
- **C17** (bound, [source](#stmt-geom-overlap)): Use the prepared data of Proposition <a href="#geom:preparation" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="geom:preparation">14</a> in dimension $j=2$ or $3$. For a query point $x$, let $\mathcal H_x$ be any family of hyperplanes through $x$ such that $$\lambda'_H:=q\|X_x\cap H\|/n'\leq2\quad(H\in\mathcal H_x).$$ For distinct $H,H'\in\mathcal H_x$ put $\theta_{HH'}=q\|X_x\cap H\cap H'\|/n'$. Let $E_a$ be the number of ordered pairs with $\theta_{HH'}\geq a$ and let $\Delta_a$ be the maximum degree of this graph. Outside $o(n)$ ambient centers, simultaneously for dyadic $a\in[q^{-10},2]$ and for every such family, $$\label{geom:overlap-bounds} E_a\leq B^2e^{2\chi}a^{-200},\qquad \Delta_a\leq Be^{2\chi}a^{-200}.$$ Let $p$ be any even integer in $[10000\sigma/P,10000\sigma/P+2]$ and put $t_*=1/(100p)$. Outside a further set of at most $ne^{CP}$ ambient centers, also $$\label{geom:strong-degree} \Delta_{t_*}\leq Be^{-3P}.$$ The further exceptional set is used only for ambient output-size control, not for support capture.
  - Explanation: Uniform pencil overlap counts and degrees follow from the prepared geometry; the larger exceptional set serves ambient-size control only.
- **C18** (construction, [source](#stmt-learn-short-row)): Fix the constants in <a href="#learn:support-hypotheses" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="learn:support-hypotheses">[learn:support-hypotheses]</a>, the bound on $b/K_*$, the bound on $\tau\sigma^{100\beta}$, and $C_0$. For every sufficiently large prime $q$, the prepared input just specified has the following description scheme. Using a fresh public table, it either declares failure or describes a set $W\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\|W\|\le n e^{CP},\qquad \|W\cap S\|\ge c n.$$ Failure has probability at most $C e^{-cq}$, and the message has at most $CqP(d_S+1)+Cq+C\sigma$ bits. In particular this is within $CqP(d_S+d_T+P)$ for any $d_T\ge0$. The bounds are uniform in all supports and prepared inputs satisfying the hypotheses, including inputs fixed by earlier random history before the fresh table is sampled. The decoder uses only $U_S$, public parameters, and the new message; it need not know $S$, $T$, or $S'$.
  - Explanation: A short public-table message describes a cap without revealing hidden supports; the statement controls length and failure, not runtime.
- **C19** (bound, [source](#stmt-learn-regular-pencils)): There is a deterministic set $D_2$ of size $o(n)$ such that, for $x\notin D_2$, its pencil contains at most $Be^\xi$ exceptional hyperplanes and satisfies $$\sum_{H\ni x}\min\{1,(\lambda_H-1)^2\}\le Be^\xi, \qquad \sum_{H\ni x}\min\{1,(\lambda_{H,C}-f_C)^2\}\le Be^\xi$$ for every cell $C$. The constants and the $o(n)$ estimate are uniform over the prepared inputs. We call these pencils regular. Moreover, for every regular pencil and its typical members, with $$\label{learn:outside-mass} \lambda'_H=\frac{q\|H\cap X_x\|}{n'},\qquad \delta'_H=\lambda'_H-(1-f_x),$$ we have $$\label{learn:typical-bounds} .75\le\lambda'_H<2,\qquad \|\delta'_H\|\le .18,\qquad \sum_{\substack{H\ni x\\H\text{ typical}}}(\delta'_H)^2 \le CBe^\xi.$$
  - Explanation: Deterministic regular pencils bound exceptional counts and squared deviations, giving normalized outside-mass bounds.
- **C20** (bound, [source](#stmt-learn-empty-exceptional)): There is a fixed $c_0>0$ such that, with probability at least $c e^{-8P\tau}$, $$\label{learn:empty-good-event} z\ge z_{\min}:=c_0qB e^{-b-8P\tau},\qquad \sum_{H\in Z}\lambda_H\le .001z.$$ For every $Z$ satisfying these two inequalities, at least $.99n'$ points of $S'$ have $z_x\le .1z/q$, whereas all but $O(q^{j+1}/z)$ ambient points have $z_x\ge .8z/q$. At a regular pencil, the exceptional-hyperplane part of <a href="#learn:score" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="learn:score">[learn:score]</a> differs from $(1-b_x)^Rz_x$ by at most $b_xBe^\xi=o(z_{\min}/q)$, uniformly in the samples, and $(1-b_x)^R=1-o(1)$.
  - Explanation: Empty exceptional hyperplanes produce distinct support and ambient score behavior on a positive-probability event.
- **C21** (bound, [source](#stmt-learn-second-moment)): If $x\notin D_0\cup D_2$, then, conditional on $\mathcal O_x$, $$\mathbb E\bigl((T_x^*)^2\mid\mathcal O_x\bigr) \le B^2e^{-.6P}.$$ This assertion does not exclude the set $D_1$.
  - Explanation: The conditional second moment controls support capture without discarding the larger ambient exceptional set.
- **C22** (bound, [source](#stmt-learn-high-moment)): If $x\notin D_0\cup D_1\cup D_2$, then, conditional on $\mathcal O_x$, $$\mathbb E\bigl((T_x^*)^p\mid\mathcal O_x\bigr) \le B^p e^{-.1pP}.$$
  - Explanation: The conditional even high moment suppresses unwanted ambient points, using static hit certificates with no double-charged events.
- **C23** (bound, [source](#stmt-learn-row-success)): For the Poisson batches sampled from $S'$, with probability at least $c e^{-8P\tau}$ their total size is at most $2qP$ and the test <a href="#learn:score" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="learn:score">[learn:score]</a>, including all sampled points, captures at least $c n$ points of $S$ and contains at most $n e^{CP}$ ambient points. The integer $z$ and the score are those determined by these same batches.
  - Explanation: True-law Poisson batches simultaneously bound sample size, retain support mass and control the decoded ambient set.
- **C24** (construction, [source](#stmt-learn-implementation)): The true-law success in Lemma <a href="#learn:row-success" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="learn:row-success">23</a> gives the description scheme of Proposition <a href="#learn:short-row" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="learn:short-row">18</a>.
  - Explanation: Finite public-table proposal search implements the true-law success event by transmitting the accepted index; it does not preserve unbiased row law.
- **C25** (construction, [source](#stmt-mark-marking)): Under <a href="#mark:stage-budget" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:stage-budget">[mark:stage-budget]</a>, both scans together mark at most $h\le Cq\sigma$ positions expensive. The old context, the two rank and type masks, and the values of all positions expensive in either scan form a message $\theta_0$ satisfying $$\label{mark:message-cost} H(\theta_0)\le\Lambda+O(k) +(d\sigma+\Delta+O(1))\mathbb Eh.$$ At a fixed $\theta_0$, every unspecified flag has a known joint domain of size at most $e^{J_0}$, where $J_0=d\sigma+O(1)$ is deterministic. For the set $U$ of unspecified slots, and every $\theta_0$-determined subset $S\subseteq U$, $$\label{mark:subset-deficit} 0\le \mathbb E\bigl[\|S\|J_0-H_{\theta_0}(F_S)\bigr] \le C(\Lambda+k+\Delta q\sigma).$$ Here $H_{\theta_0}$ means entropy in the conditional law at the specified value of $\theta_0$. Independently of the extraction entropy lower bound, the same message gives $$\label{mark:entropy-upper} H(F)\le d\sigma\ell+\Lambda+O(k)+O(\Delta q\sigma).$$
  - Explanation: Forward and dual-reverse marking bounds expensive slots and cheap joint domains, yielding the message and entropy budgets.
- **C26** (bound, [source](#stmt-mark-representatives)): There is a deterministic round $t<T_1$, depending only on the current joint law, such that, with $\theta=\theta_t$, $U=U_t$, and $E=E_t$, $$\label{mark:representative-bounds} \begin{aligned} \mathbb E\sum_{i\in U}\delta_i&\le\mathcal B,& \mathbb E\sum_{i\in U}M_i&\le C\mathcal B/T_1,\\ \mathbb E\sum_{i\in E}\delta_i&\le C\mathcal B/m,& \mathbb E\sum_{i\in E}M_i&\le C\mathcal B/(mT_1). \end{aligned}$$ All four expectations refer to the same pre-round history and fresh indices. No values $F_E$ have yet been exposed.
  - Explanation: A law-determined exposure round gives simultaneous expected deficit and representative-information bounds before fresh flags are exposed.
- **C27** (bound, [source](#stmt-mark-endpoint-mass)): At every fixed history and unexposed slot $i$, the probability that either specified endpoint is not good is at most $$\label{mark:bad-endpoints} C\left(e^{-K}+\frac{\delta_i+1}{K}\right).$$ This holds for both reciprocal and high classes, with their stated marginal caps.
  - Explanation: Good-endpoint failure mass is controlled by entropy deficit, heavy-atom tests and marginal caps.
- **C28** (bound, [source](#stmt-mark-collision)): Fix a history $\theta$ in the selected pre-round. For unexposed slots $i<j$ in a reciprocal class, write their flags as $(a,b)$ and $(c,y)$. There are symmetric nonnegative numbers $c_{ij}=c_{ji}$, with $c_{ii}=0$, such that $$\label{mark:incidence-charge} \mathbb P_{\mathrm{prod}}(a,y\text{ good},\ a\perp y) \le C(I_{ij}+c_{ij}), \qquad \sum_{i<j}c_{ij}\le C\sigma k.$$ Here the product law is the product of the two conditional flag marginals at $\theta$. In an open band $c_{ij}=0$. In an integer band, $c_{ij}$ is the conditional probability of a specified pair event placing both flags in one rectangle. For high classes set $c_{ij}=0$; no incidence estimate is asserted for them.
  - Explanation: Reciprocal product-law incidence is bounded by mutual information and adaptive rectangle charges; open bands have no charge.
- **C29** (bound, [source](#stmt-mark-good-indices)): In every selected class, the expected proportion of bad unexposed indices is $o(1)$, and so is the expected proportion of bad fresh representatives. At every good index each endpoint is good with probability $1-o(1)$, uniformly over the history and fresh indices. All limits hold for fixed $d,\eta$ and the stage constants.
  - Explanation: Bad indices and fresh representatives have vanishing expected fractions, and good indices have uniformly small bad-endpoint mass.
- **C30** (construction, [source](#stmt-comp-step)): Let $F$ be a consistent tuple of deterministic length $c_0k\le \ell\le k$, for a fixed $c_0>0$, extracted in one of the two orientations from the flag stream. Suppose that the stream law is dominated by a fixed constant times its original independent law and is supported on the rectangle-occupancy event of Lemma <a href="#base:rectangles" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="base:rectangles">5</a>. Let a discrete context $\mathsf C$ specify a domain for each slot, with $$H(\mathsf C)\le\Lambda,\qquad \|\text{domain of each slot}\|\le Cq^d e^\Delta, \qquad \Lambda,\Delta\ge0.$$ Define, as in <a href="#mark:parameters" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="mark:parameters">[mark:parameters]</a>, $$D=\sigma^\beta(1+\Lambda/k+\Delta\sigma^{-\eta}), \qquad \sigma^\beta\le D\le\sigma^{1-\eta/2}.$$ There is an extraction of a consistent tuple $F'$ of deterministic length $\ell'=\Theta(k)$, still in one of the two stream orientations, and a new discrete context $\mathsf C'$ for which $$\label{comp:step-bounds} \begin{split} H(\mathsf C')&\le\Lambda' \le CqP\log(w+2)(\sigma+wP)+Cw\sigma,\\ \|\text{domain of each slot of }F'\| &\le Cq^d e^{\Delta'},\qquad \Delta'\le CK_*,\\ &\hspace{1em}1\le w\le C\sigma^{1+\eta/2}/D. \end{split}$$ Here $w$ is a deterministic integer fixed during the preparation of the step. The new decoder uses only $\mathsf C'$, fixed public parameters, and fixed public tables; it does not use $\mathsf C$. The extraction is obtained by conditioning on an event of probability bounded below by a positive constant. Consequently its stream law still has constant domination and satisfies the same occupancy bound. All constants, including the retained proportion, are independent of $q$. They and the sufficiently-large-$q$ threshold are uniform over the input laws and contexts: they depend only on $d,\eta,c_0$ and fixed numerical bounds for the domination constant and the domain prefactor, not on the realized history or on $\Lambda,\Delta$ within the stated admissible range.
  - Explanation: A standalone replacement context retains a fixed-length consistent subtuple after constant-probability conditioning, without retaining the old context.
- **C31** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-rich-union)): Write $\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(d,q)=\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(\mathbb F_q^{d+1})$. Let $X\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(d,q)$ and $0<\delta\le1$. Let $\mathcal V$ be any family of nonzero vector subspaces of $\mathbb F_q^{d+1}$ satisfying $$\|X\cap\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(V)\|\ge\delta\|\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(V)\|\qquad(V\in\mathcal V).$$ Then $$\left\|\bigcup_{V\in\mathcal V}\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(V)\right\| \le C_d\delta^{-(d+1)}\|X\|.$$
  - Explanation: A finite-degree evaluation-rank argument bounds the union of subspaces rich in a given projective point set.
- **C32** (construction, [source](#stmt-comp-high-rank)): Suppose the preparation in Section <a href="#mark:section" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="mark:section">5</a> produces a high-rank class with fixed vector ranks $r,r'$, where $r+r'>d+1$, and one window of length $\Theta(k)$. There is a new context of length $O(q\sigma^{1+\beta})$ bits whose decoded flag domain has size at most $q^d e^{O(K)}$. With probability bounded below, this domain contains a deterministic number $\Theta(k)$ of middle targets, retained in their original order. The decoder does not need the old history or any conditional law.
  - Explanation: Two independent auxiliary rows construct a bounded high-rank flag domain retaining a fixed number of middle targets; the decoder uses raw rows only.
- **C33** (construction, [source](#stmt-comp-supports)): At every good index in a reciprocal class, there are auxiliary choices of first and second supports consisting entirely of good endpoints. If $A$ is the first support and $p_i^A$ the conditional first-endpoint law, then $$\label{comp:support-measure} \tfrac12 A_{\max,i}e^{-K}\le \|A\|\le A_{\max,i}, \qquad \mathbb E\frac{\boldsymbol 1_{\{a\in A\}}}{\|A\|} \le C p_i^A(a).$$ The analogous assertions hold for the second support $B$. All such support choices can be made mutually independently, conditional on the history and indices.
  - Explanation: Independent auxiliary endpoint supports have size lower bounds and averaged uniform measures dominated pointwise by original marginal laws.
- **C34** (bound, [source](#stmt-comp-tree-loss)): The expected number of middle targets lost through the pre-tree deletions, deleted subtrees, or exclusion by the produced caps is $o(k)$. In particular, a prescribed deterministic number $\Theta(k)$ of targets can be retained in order with probability bounded below.
  - Explanation: Unconditioned readiness estimates and chronological incidence bound expected tree and cap losses, enabling deterministic ordered retention.
- **C35** (bound, [source](#stmt-end-prime-interval)): There is a constant $c_0>0$ such that, for every sufficiently large real $x$, a prime $q$ satisfies $c_0x\le q\le x$.
  - Explanation: Elementary Chebyshev estimates provide a prime in a fixed-ratio interval for every sufficiently large real scale.
- **C36** (bound, [source](#stmt-end-triangle-free)): If a triangle-free graph $H$ has $n>0$ vertices and maximum degree at most a real number $D_0\ge1$, then $$\alpha(H)\ge\frac{n\log(D_0+1)}{8(D_0+1)}.$$
  - Explanation: Uniform independent-set exposure and Jensen yield a finite triangle-free independence lower bound with the stated constant.
- **C37** (bound, [source](#stmt-end-upper-bound)): For every fixed integer $s\ge2$ there is a constant $C_s$ such that for all sufficiently large integers $t$, $$r(s,t)\le C_s\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{s-2}}.$$
  - Explanation: Induction on clique order combines neighborhood bounds, vertex sampling and triangle deletion to prove the classical logarithmic upper estimate.

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-base-incidence",
        "exact_quote": "Let $j\\ge2$, and put $p_j=Q_{j-1}/Q_j=(1-Q_j^{-1})/q$. For any real weights $w_x$ on one of the two projective spaces, $$\\sum_y\\left(\\sum_{x\\perp y}w_x-p_j\\sum_xw_x\\right)^2\n =q^{j-1}\\left(\\sum_xw_x^2-\\frac{(\\sum_xw_x)^2}{Q_j}\\right)\n \\le q^{j-1}\\sum_xw_x^2.\n \\label{base:weighted-variance}$$ In particular, for sets $S,T$ in the opposite spaces, $$\\left|e(S,T)-p_j|S||T|\\right|\n \\le q^{(j-1)/2}\\sqrt{|S||T|}.\n \\label{base:mixing}$$ If $S,T$ are nonempty and $e(S,T)/(|S||T|)=o(1/q)$, then $|S||T|\\le Cq^{j+1}$ for all sufficiently large $q$.",

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-description",
        "exact_quote": "Let $2\\leq j\\leq d$. Let $S,T$ be nonempty sets in opposite point systems of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(j,q)$, contained in decoder-known sets $U_S,U_T$. Suppose, for a fixed constant $C_b$, that $$\\label{geom:sparse-hypotheses}\n |S||T|\\geq q^{j+1}e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{e(S,T)}{|S||T|}\\leq\\frac{\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n 0\\leq b\\leq C_bK_*,\\quad\n 0<\\tau\\leq C_b\\sigma^{-100\\beta}.$$ Write $$d_S=\\log\\frac{|U_S|}{|S|},\\qquad\n d_T=\\log\\frac{|U_T|}{|T|},$$ and orient the systems so that $n=|S|\\leq |T|$. There is a public-table description, of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P),$$ which, except with probability $Ce^{-cq}$, produces a decoder-known set $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\\label{geom:description-output}\n |W|\\leq ne^{CP},\\qquad |W\\cap S|\\geq cn.$$ The probability bound holds conditional on any earlier data independent of the tables used by this invocation.",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geom-description",
          "exact_quote": "Let $2\\leq j\\leq d$. Let $S,T$ be nonempty sets in opposite point systems of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(j,q)$, contained in decoder-known sets $U_S,U_T$. Suppose, for a fixed constant $C_b$, that $$\\label{geom:sparse-hypotheses}\n |S||T|\\geq q^{j+1}e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{e(S,T)}{|S||T|}\\leq\\frac{\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n 0\\leq b\\leq C_bK_*,\\quad\n 0<\\tau\\leq C_b\\sigma^{-100\\beta}.$$ Write $$d_S=\\log\\frac{|U_S|}{|S|},\\qquad\n d_T=\\log\\frac{|U_T|}{|T|},$$ and orient the systems so that $n=|S|\\leq |T|$. There is a public-table description, of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P),$$ which, except with probability $Ce^{-cq}$, produces a decoder-known set $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\\label{geom:description-output}\n |W|\\leq ne^{CP},\\qquad |W\\cap S|\\geq cn.$$ The probability bound holds conditional on any earlier data independent of the tables used by this invocation.",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $2\\leq j\\leq d$. Let $S,T$ be nonempty sets in opposite point systems of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(j,q)$, contained in decoder-known sets $U_S,U_T$. There is a public-table description, of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P),$$ which, except with probability $Ce^{-cq}$, produces a decoder-known set $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\\label{geom:description-output}\n |W|\\leq ne^{CP},\\qquad |W\\cap S|\\geq cn.$$ The probability bound holds conditional on any earlier data independent of the tables used by this invocation.",

There is a public-table description, of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P),$$ which, except with probability $Ce^{-cq}$, produces a decoder-known set $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\\label{geom:description-output}\n |W|\\leq ne^{CP},\\qquad |W\\cap S|\\geq cn.$$ The probability bound holds conditional on any earlier data independent of the tables used by this invocation.",

        "anchor": "stmt-geom-validation",
        "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#geom:sparse-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:sparse-hypotheses\">[geom:sparse-hypotheses]</a>, suppose a decoder-known $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfies <a href=\"#geom:description-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:description-output\">[geom:description-output]</a>. Further independent tables and a message of length $CqP(d_S+d_T+1)$ produce caps $U_T'\\subseteq U_T$ and $U_S'\\subseteq U_S$, with additional failure probability $Ce^{-cq}$, such that $$\\label{geom:validation-output}\n \\begin{aligned}\n |U_T'|&\\leq \\frac{Cq^{j+1}}{|S|},&\n |U_S'|&\\leq \\frac{Cq^{j+1}}{|T|},\\\\\n |U_T'\\cap T|&\\geq .99|T|,&\n |U_S'\\cap S|&\\geq .99|S|.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Each cap is its input domain intersected with an ambient test: a candidate passes if it is incident with fewer than $h/(5q)$ entries of the transmitted row of length $h$ in the opposite system.",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"
      },

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-produced-test",
        "exact_quote": "Consider either test in Lemma <a href=\"#geom:validation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:validation\">11</a>, conditional on all data making that test ready, but not on any later event. Suppose its hidden source $X_0$ is a subset of an original support $X$ with $|X_0|\\geq c|X|$. For every fixed opposite point $y$, $$\\label{geom:exclusion-bound}\n \\mathbb P\\{\\text{the test is produced and its ambient test excludes }y\\}\n \\leq Cq\\frac{|X\\cap N(y)|}{|X|}.$$ Conditional on production, the row law is dominated by a constant times the independent uniform row law on $X_0$. The bound <a href=\"#geom:exclusion-bound\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:exclusion-bound\">[geom:exclusion-bound]</a> may be averaged over earlier readiness data whenever its right-hand side is fixed.",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-geom-preparation",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose <a href=\"#geom:sparse-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:sparse-hypotheses\">[geom:sparse-hypotheses]</a> holds with $j\\in\\{2,3\\}$ and $n=|S|\\leq|T|$. Either enumeration already proves Lemma <a href=\"#geom:description\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:description\">10</a>, or the following preparation costs $O(q+\\sigma)$ bits. In dimension three there is also a possible reduction to the dimension-two description lemma, with the same final cost, size, capture, and failure bounds as Lemma <a href=\"#geom:description\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:description\">10</a>.*\n\n*In the case not resolved by enumeration or that reduction, we have $n>100qP$, a retained set $S'\\subseteq S$ of size $n'\\in[n/2,n]$, and either an empty list or an ordered plane list $\\Pi_1,\\ldots,\\Pi_J$ in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(3,q)$. In the latter case the sets $$\\label{geom:cells}\n C_i=(S\\cap\\Pi_i)\\setminus\\bigcup_{h<i}\\Pi_h\n \\quad(1\\leq i\\leq J)$$ partition $S'$, have nonincreasing sizes, and satisfy $|C_i|<.04n'$. The decoder knows the list and all cell sizes, but need not know $S'$ or the cells themselves.*\n\n\n*Write $$\\label{geom:lowdim-parameters}\n n=q^{1+v},\\quad g=(v-1/2)\\sigma,\\quad\n \\chi=P/100,\\quad\\chi_0=P/10000,\\quad B=q^j/n'.$$ For every ambient query point $x$, define $O_x$ to be the cell $C_i$ of its earliest containing listed plane, and $O_x=\\varnothing$ if there is none. Define $$\\label{geom:outside-support}\n f_x=|O_x|/n'\\leq .04,\\qquad\n X_x=S'\\setminus(O_x\\cup\\{x\\}).$$ If no list is present, $O_x=\\varnothing$ for all $x$. When $j=3$ and $g>\\chi_0$, the preparation additionally ensures either*\n\n1.  *every plane meets $S'$ in fewer than $K_p=n^{4/3}q^{-1}e^{-g/5}$ points and the list is empty; or*\n\n2.  *the list is present and $J\\leq n/K_p=q^{1/2}e^{-2g/15}$.",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geom-preparation",
          "exact_quote": "Suppose <a href=\"#geom:sparse-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:sparse-hypotheses\">[geom:sparse-hypotheses]</a> holds with $j\\in\\{2,3\\}$ and $n=|S|\\leq|T|$. Either enumeration already proves Lemma <a href=\"#geom:description\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:description\">10</a>, or the following preparation costs $O(q+\\sigma)$ bits. In the latter case the sets $$\\label{geom:cells}\n C_i=(S\\cap\\Pi_i)\\setminus\\bigcup_{h<i}\\Pi_h\n \\quad(1\\leq i\\leq J)$$ partition $S'$, have nonincreasing sizes, and satisfy $|C_i|<.04n'$. Define $$\\label{geom:outside-support}\n f_x=|O_x|/n'\\leq .04,\\qquad\n X_x=S'\\setminus(O_x\\cup\\{x\\}).$$ If no list is present, $O_x=\\varnothing$ for all $x$. When $j=3$ and $g>\\chi_0$, the preparation additionally ensures either*\n\n1.  *every plane meets $S'$ in fewer than $K_p=n^{4/3}q^{-1}e^{-g/5}$ points and the list is empty; or*\n\n2.  *the list is present and $J\\leq n/K_p=q^{1/2}e^{-2g/15}$.",

      "exact_statement": "Suppose <a href=\"#geom:sparse-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:sparse-hypotheses\">[geom:sparse-hypotheses]</a> holds with $j\\in\\{2,3\\}$ and $n=|S|\\leq|T|$. In dimension three there is also a possible reduction to the dimension-two description lemma, with the same final cost, size, capture, and failure bounds as Lemma <a href=\"#geom:description\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:description\">10</a>.*\n\n*In the case not resolved by enumeration or that reduction, we have $n>100qP$, a retained set $S'\\subseteq S$ of size $n'\\in[n/2,n]$, and either an empty list or an ordered plane list $\\Pi_1,\\ldots,\\Pi_J$ in $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(3,q)$. In the latter case the sets $$\\label{geom:cells}\n C_i=(S\\cap\\Pi_i)\\setminus\\bigcup_{h<i}\\Pi_h\n \\quad(1\\leq i\\leq J)$$ partition $S'$, have nonincreasing sizes, and satisfy $|C_i|<.04n'$. The decoder knows the list and all cell sizes, but need not know $S'$ or the cells themselves.*\n\n\n*Write $$\\label{geom:lowdim-parameters}\n n=q^{1+v},\\quad g=(v-1/2)\\sigma,\\quad\n \\chi=P/100,\\quad\\chi_0=P/10000,\\quad B=q^j/n'.$$ For every ambient query point $x$, define $O_x$ to be the cell $C_i$ of its earliest containing listed plane, and $O_x=\\varnothing$ if there is none. Define $$\\label{geom:outside-support}\n f_x=|O_x|/n'\\leq .04,\\qquad\n X_x=S'\\setminus(O_x\\cup\\{x\\}).$$ If no list is present, $O_x=\\varnothing$ for all $x$. When $j=3$ and $g>\\chi_0$, the preparation additionally ensures either*\n\n1.  *every plane meets $S'$ in fewer than $K_p=n^{4/3}q^{-1}e^{-g/5}$ points and the list is empty; or*\n\n2.  *the list is present and $J\\leq n/K_p=q^{1/2}e^{-2g/15}$.",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"
        }
      ],
      "statement_anchor": "stmt-geom-preparation"
    },

        "exact_quote": "Let $q$ run through sufficiently large primes. Let $\\mathcal S$ be at most $n\\leq Cq^2$ points of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(3,q)$, with at most $K_p$ points in every projective plane. Let $M>0$ satisfy $$\\label{geom:rich-regime}\n \\frac{M^3}{n}\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n \\frac{M^2}{K_p}\\longrightarrow\\infty,\\qquad\n cq\\leq\\frac nM\\leq Cq^{41/40}.$$ Then at most $Cn/M$ projective lines contain at least $M$ points of $\\mathcal S$.         "anchor": "stmt-geom-overlap",
        "exact_quote": "Use the prepared data of Proposition <a href=\"#geom:preparation\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"geom:preparation\">14</a> in dimension $j=2$ or $3$. For a query point $x$, let $\\mathcal H_x$ be any family of hyperplanes through $x$ such that $$\\lambda'_H:=q|X_x\\cap H|/n'\\leq2\\quad(H\\in\\mathcal H_x).$$ For distinct $H,H'\\in\\mathcal H_x$ put $\\theta_{HH'}=q|X_x\\cap H\\cap H'|/n'$. Let $E_a$ be the number of ordered pairs with $\\theta_{HH'}\\geq a$ and let $\\Delta_a$ be the maximum degree of this graph. Outside $o(n)$ ambient centers, simultaneously for dyadic $a\\in[q^{-10},2]$ and for every such family, $$\\label{geom:overlap-bounds}\n E_a\\leq B^2e^{2\\chi}a^{-200},\\qquad\n \\Delta_a\\leq Be^{2\\chi}a^{-200}.$$ Let $p$ be any even integer in $[10000\\sigma/P,10000\\sigma/P+2]$ and put $t_*=1/(100p)$. Outside a further set of at most $ne^{CP}$ ambient centers, also $$\\label{geom:strong-degree}\n \\Delta_{t_*}\\leq Be^{-3P}.$$ The further exceptional set is used only for ambient output-size control, not for support capture.",

        "exact_quote": "Fix the constants in <a href=\"#learn:support-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"learn:support-hypotheses\">[learn:support-hypotheses]</a>, the bound on $b/K_*$, the bound on $\\tau\\sigma^{100\\beta}$, and $C_0$. For every sufficiently large prime $q$, the prepared input just specified has the following description scheme. Using a fresh public table, it either declares failure or describes a set $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$|W|\\le n e^{CP},\\qquad |W\\cap S|\\ge c n.$$ Failure has probability at most $C e^{-cq}$, and the message has at most $CqP(d_S+1)+Cq+C\\sigma$ bits. In particular this is within $CqP(d_S+d_T+P)$ for any $d_T\\ge0$. The bounds are uniform in all supports and prepared inputs satisfying the hypotheses, including inputs fixed by earlier random history before the fresh table is sampled. The decoder uses only $U_S$, public parameters, and the new message; it need not know $S$, $T$, or $S'$.",

      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-learn-regular-pencils",
        "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic set $D_2$ of size $o(n)$ such that, for $x\\notin D_2$, its pencil contains at most $Be^\\xi$ exceptional hyperplanes and satisfies $$\\sum_{H\\ni x}\\min\\{1,(\\lambda_H-1)^2\\}\\le Be^\\xi,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{H\\ni x}\\min\\{1,(\\lambda_{H,C}-f_C)^2\\}\\le Be^\\xi$$ for every cell $C$. The constants and the $o(n)$ estimate are uniform over the prepared inputs. We call these pencils regular. Moreover, for every regular pencil and its typical members, with $$\\label{learn:outside-mass}\n \\lambda'_H=\\frac{q|H\\cap X_x|}{n'},\\qquad\n \\delta'_H=\\lambda'_H-(1-f_x),$$ we have $$\\label{learn:typical-bounds}\n .75\\le\\lambda'_H<2,\\qquad |\\delta'_H|\\le .18,\\qquad\n \\sum_{\\substack{H\\ni x\\\\H\\text{ typical}}}(\\delta'_H)^2\n \\le CBe^\\xi.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-learn-regular-pencils",
          "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic set $D_2$ of size $o(n)$ such that, for $x\\notin D_2$, its pencil contains at most $Be^\\xi$ exceptional hyperplanes and satisfies $$\\sum_{H\\ni x}\\min\\{1,(\\lambda_H-1)^2\\}\\le Be^\\xi,\n \\qquad\n \\sum_{H\\ni x}\\min\\{1,(\\lambda_{H,C}-f_C)^2\\}\\le Be^\\xi$$ for every cell $C$. The constants and the $o(n)$ estimate are uniform over the prepared inputs. We call these pencils regular. Moreover, for every regular pencil and its typical members, with $$\\label{learn:outside-mass}\n \\lambda'_H=\\frac{q|H\\cap X_x|}{n'},\\qquad\n \\delta'_H=\\lambda'_H-(1-f_x),$$ we have $$\\label{learn:typical-bounds}\n .75\\le\\lambda'_H<2,\\qquad |\\delta'_H|\\le .18,\\qquad\n \\sum_{\\substack{H\\ni x\\\\H\\text{ typical}}}(\\delta'_H)^2\n \\le CBe^\\xi.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"

    {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-learn-empty-exceptional",
        "exact_quote": "There is a fixed $c_0>0$ such that, with probability at least $c e^{-8P\\tau}$, $$\\label{learn:empty-good-event}\n z\\ge z_{\\min}:=c_0qB e^{-b-8P\\tau},\\qquad\n \\sum_{H\\in Z}\\lambda_H\\le .001z.$$ For every $Z$ satisfying these two inequalities, at least $.99n'$ points of $S'$ have $z_x\\le .1z/q$, whereas all but $O(q^{j+1}/z)$ ambient points have $z_x\\ge .8z/q$.         "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#mark:stage-budget\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"mark:stage-budget\">[mark:stage-budget]</a>, both scans together mark at most $h\\le Cq\\sigma$ positions expensive. The old context, the two rank and type masks, and the values of all positions expensive in either scan form a message $\\theta_0$ satisfying $$\\label{mark:message-cost}\n H(\\theta_0)\\le\\Lambda+O(k)\n       +(d\\sigma+\\Delta+O(1))\\mathbb Eh.$$ At a fixed $\\theta_0$, every unspecified flag has a known joint domain of size at most $e^{J_0}$, where $J_0=d\\sigma+O(1)$ is deterministic. For the set $U$ of unspecified slots, and every $\\theta_0$-determined subset $S\\subseteq U$, $$\\label{mark:subset-deficit}\n 0\\le \\mathbb E\\bigl[|S|J_0-H_{\\theta_0}(F_S)\\bigr]\n       \\le C(\\Lambda+k+\\Delta q\\sigma).$$ Here $H_{\\theta_0}$ means entropy in the conditional law at the specified value of $\\theta_0$. Independently of the extraction entropy lower bound, the same message gives $$\\label{mark:entropy-upper}\n H(F)\\le d\\sigma\\ell+\\Lambda+O(k)+O(\\Delta q\\sigma).$$",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-mark-representatives",
        "exact_quote": "There is a deterministic round $t<T_1$, depending only on the current joint law, such that, with $\\theta=\\theta_t$, $U=U_t$, and $E=E_t$, $$\\label{mark:representative-bounds}\n \\begin{aligned}\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{i\\in U}\\delta_i&\\le\\mathcal B,&\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{i\\in U}M_i&\\le C\\mathcal B/T_1,\\\\\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{i\\in E}\\delta_i&\\le C\\mathcal B/m,&\n \\mathbb E\\sum_{i\\in E}M_i&\\le C\\mathcal B/(mT_1).\n \\end{aligned}$$ All four expectations refer to the same pre-round history and fresh indices.     {
      "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-mark-collision",
        "exact_quote": "Fix a history $\\theta$ in the selected pre-round. For unexposed slots $i<j$ in a reciprocal class, write their flags as $(a,b)$ and $(c,y)$. There are symmetric nonnegative numbers $c_{ij}=c_{ji}$, with $c_{ii}=0$, such that $$\\label{mark:incidence-charge}\n \\mathbb P_{\\mathrm{prod}}(a,y\\text{ good},\\ a\\perp y)\n                   \\le C(I_{ij}+c_{ij}),\n \\qquad \\sum_{i<j}c_{ij}\\le C\\sigma k.$$ Here the product law is the product of the two conditional flag marginals at $\\theta$. In an open band $c_{ij}=0$. In an integer band, $c_{ij}$ is the conditional probability of a specified pair event placing both flags in one rectangle. For high classes set $c_{ij}=0$; no incidence estimate is asserted for them.",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"
      },
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-mark-collision",
          "exact_quote": "Fix a history $\\theta$ in the selected pre-round. For unexposed slots $i<j$ in a reciprocal class, write their flags as $(a,b)$ and $(c,y)$. There are symmetric nonnegative numbers $c_{ij}=c_{ji}$, with $c_{ii}=0$, such that $$\\label{mark:incidence-charge}\n \\mathbb P_{\\mathrm{prod}}(a,y\\text{ good},\\ a\\perp y)\n                   \\le C(I_{ij}+c_{ij}),\n \\qquad \\sum_{i<j}c_{ij}\\le C\\sigma k.$$ Here the product law is the product of the two conditional flag marginals at $\\theta$. In an open band $c_{ij}=0$. In an integer band, $c_{ij}$ is the conditional probability of a specified pair event placing both flags in one rectangle. For high classes set $c_{ij}=0$; no incidence estimate is asserted for them.",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"
        }
      ],

        "anchor": "stmt-mark-good-indices",
        "exact_quote": "In every selected class, the expected proportion of bad unexposed indices is $o(1)$, and so is the expected proportion of bad fresh representatives.       "conclusion": {
        "anchor": "stmt-comp-step",
        "exact_quote": "Let $F$ be a consistent tuple of deterministic length $c_0k\\le \\ell\\le k$, for a fixed $c_0>0$, extracted in one of the two orientations from the flag stream. Suppose that the stream law is dominated by a fixed constant times its original independent law and is supported on the rectangle-occupancy event of Lemma <a href=\"#base:rectangles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"base:rectangles\">5</a>. Let a discrete context $\\mathsf C$ specify a domain for each slot, with $$H(\\mathsf C)\\le\\Lambda,\\qquad\n |\\text{domain of each slot}|\\le Cq^d e^\\Delta,\n \\qquad \\Lambda,\\Delta\\ge0.$$ Define, as in <a href=\"#mark:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"mark:parameters\">[mark:parameters]</a>, $$D=\\sigma^\\beta(1+\\Lambda/k+\\Delta\\sigma^{-\\eta}),\n \\qquad \\sigma^\\beta\\le D\\le\\sigma^{1-\\eta/2}.$$ There is an extraction of a consistent tuple $F'$ of deterministic length $\\ell'=\\Theta(k)$, still in one of the two stream orientations, and a new discrete context $\\mathsf C'$ for which $$\\label{comp:step-bounds}\n\\begin{split}\n H(\\mathsf C')&\\le\\Lambda'\n     \\le CqP\\log(w+2)(\\sigma+wP)+Cw\\sigma,\\\\\n |\\text{domain of each slot of }F'|\n     &\\le Cq^d e^{\\Delta'},\\qquad\n       \\Delta'\\le CK_*,\\\\\n &\\hspace{1em}1\\le w\\le C\\sigma^{1+\\eta/2}/D.\n\\end{split}$$ Here $w$ is a deterministic integer fixed during the preparation of the step. The new decoder uses only $\\mathsf C'$, fixed public parameters, and fixed public tables; it does not use $\\mathsf C$. The extraction is obtained by conditioning on an event of probability bounded below by a positive constant. Consequently its stream law still has constant domination and satisfies the same occupancy bound. All constants, including the retained proportion, are independent of $q$. They and the sufficiently-large-$q$ threshold are uniform over the input laws and contexts: they depend only on $d,\\eta,c_0$ and fixed numerical bounds for the domination constant and the domain prefactor, not on the realized history or on $\\Lambda,\\Delta$ within the stated admissible range.",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-rich-union",
        "exact_quote": "Write $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(d,q)=\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(\\mathbb F_q^{d+1})$. Let $X\\subseteq\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(d,q)$ and $0<\\delta\\le1$. Let $\\mathcal V$ be any family of nonzero vector subspaces of $\\mathbb F_q^{d+1}$ satisfying $$|X\\cap\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(V)|\\ge\\delta|\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(V)|\\qquad(V\\in\\mathcal V).$$ Then $$\\left|\\bigcup_{V\\in\\mathcal V}\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(V)\\right|\n       \\le C_d\\delta^{-(d+1)}|X|.$$",
        "source_id": "PM-170.2"
      },

        "anchor": "stmt-comp-high-rank",
        "exact_quote": "Suppose the preparation in Section <a href=\"#mark:section\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"mark:section\">5</a> produces a high-rank class with fixed vector ranks $r,r'$, where $r+r'>d+1$, and one window of length $\\Theta(k)$. There is a new context of length $O(q\\sigma^{1+\\beta})$ bits whose decoded flag domain has size at most $q^d e^{O(K)}$. With probability bounded below, this domain contains a deterministic number $\\Theta(k)$ of middle targets, retained in their original order. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Ramsey number r(s,t)** ([source](#intro:section)): For integers $s,t\ge2$, the Ramsey number $r(s,t)$ is the least integer $N$ such that every finite simple graph on $N$ vertices contains a clique of order $s$ or an independent set of order $t$.
- **Natural logarithm** ([source](#intro:section)): All logarithms are natural.
- **Independence number** ([source](#pm-h-4)): For a graph $G$, write $\alpha(G)$ for its independence number.
- **Projective point** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A point of $\mathop{\mathrm{PG}}(d,q)$ is a one-dimensional vector subspace of $\mathbb F_q^{d+1}$.
- **Dual point** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A dual point is a one-dimensional subspace of the dual vector space; it specifies a projective hyperplane.
- **Incident flag** ([source](#pm-h-4)): An incident flag is a pair $(a,b)$ with $a\perp b$.
- **Vector dimension convention** ([source](#pm-h-9)): All dimensions of linear spans below are *vector* dimensions; a vector subspace of dimension $r\ge1$ has $Q_{r-1}$ projective points.
- **Ordered flag graph** ([source](#base:edge-rule)): ij\in E(G)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad a_i\perp b_j\ \hbox{ and }\ a_j\not\perp b_i.
- **Consistent tuple** ([source](#base:consistency)): i<j,\quad a_i\perp b_j\quad\Longrightarrow\quad a_j\perp b_i.
- **Vertices as positions** ([source](#base:edge-rule)): Repeated flags are allowed: graph vertices are positions, not flag values.
- **Subspace rectangle** ([source](#pm-h-10)): \mathcal R(U)= \{(a,b):b\subseteq U,\ a\subseteq U^\perp\}.
- **Shannon entropy** ([source](#pm-h-11)): For a discrete random variable $X$ with mass function $p$, write $H(X)=-\sum_xp(x)\log p(x)$, with $0\log0=0$.
- **KL divergence** ([source](#pm-h-11)): where $\mathop{\mathrm{KL}}(P\Vert Q)=\sum_xP(x)\log(P(x)/Q(x))$ is infinite if $P$ is not absolutely continuous with respect to $Q$.
- **Public random tables** ([source](#pm-h-14)): Given a decoder-known finite set, a table provides independent rows of uniform samples from that set.

| Q01 | r(s,t) | ≥ | $\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{s-2+\varepsilon}}$ | Fixed integer s >= 6; every epsilon > 0; sufficiently large integer t. The threshold is ABSENT. |

| Q03 | \|V(G)\| | = | $\lfloor q^d\sigma\rfloor$ | d >= 5, 0 < eta < 1/10; sigma = log q; sufficiently large prime q. |
| Q04 | \alpha(G) | < | $\lfloor q\sigma^{1+\eta}\rfloor$ | Existential graph for sufficiently large prime q; d and eta fixed. |
| Q05 | incidence error | ≤ | $q^{(j-1)/2}\sqrt{\|S\|\|T\|}$ | j >= 2; sets in opposite projective point systems. |
| Q06 | H(F) | ≥ | $d\sigma\ell+\eta\ell\log\sigma-O(k)$ | Constant stream-law domination; deterministic ell = Theta(k); permitted stream orientations. |
| Q07 | \beta | = | $\eta/10^7$ | 0 < eta < 1/10. |
| Q08 | K | = | $D\sigma^{3\beta}$ | sigma = log q; sigma^beta <= D <= sigma^(1-eta/2); R is deterministic and even. |
| Q09 | K_* | = | $D\sigma^{6\beta}$ | sigma = log q; sigma^beta <= D <= sigma^(1-eta/2); R is deterministic and even. |
| Q10 | L | = | $D\sigma^{8\beta}$ | sigma = log q; sigma^beta <= D <= sigma^(1-eta/2); R is deterministic and even. |
| Q11 | P | = | $LR$ | sigma = log q; sigma^beta <= D <= sigma^(1-eta/2); R is deterministic and even. |
| Q12 | P | ≤ | $4D\sigma^{9\beta}$ | Allowed D and R interval; sufficiently large q. |
| Q13 | description length | ≤ | $CqP(d_S+d_T+P)$ | 2 <= j <= d; decoder-known supersets; sparse-pair product, density and parameter hypotheses. Numeric C is ABSENT. |
| Q14 | \|U_T'\cap T\|/\|T\| | ≥ | $.99$ | Sparse hypotheses and successful description; fresh independent tables. |
| Q15 | \mathbb E((T_x^*)^2\mid\mathcal O_x) | ≤ | $B^2e^{-.6P}$ | Prepared low-dimensional inputs; x outside D_0 union D_2; readiness conditioning only. |
| Q16 | \mathbb E((T_x^*)^p\mid\mathcal O_x) | ≤ | $B^p e^{-.1pP}$ | Even prescribed p; x outside D_0 union D_1 union D_2; prepared inputs. |
| Q17 | D_new | ≤ | $\sigma^{2\beta}+D\sigma^{-\eta/4}$ | Admissible compression stage; sufficiently large q; fixed dimension and eta. |
| Q18 | T | = | $\lceil8/\eta\rceil$ | Fixed eta in (0,1/10); chosen before iterations. |
| Q19 | \alpha(H) | ≥ | $\frac{n\log(D_0+1)}{8(D_0+1)}$ | Triangle-free finite graph, n > 0; real maximum-degree bound D_0 >= 1. |
| Q20 | r(s,t) | ≤ | $C_s\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{s-2}}$ | Every fixed integer s >= 2; all sufficiently large integer t; numerical C_s and threshold are ABSENT. |

      "effectivity": "effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-base-incidence",
          "exact_quote": "Let $j\\ge2$, and put $p_j=Q_{j-1}/Q_j=(1-Q_j^{-1})/q$. For any real weights $w_x$ on one of the two projective spaces, $$\\sum_y\\left(\\sum_{x\\perp y}w_x-p_j\\sum_xw_x\\right)^2\n =q^{j-1}\\left(\\sum_xw_x^2-\\frac{(\\sum_xw_x)^2}{Q_j}\\right)\n \\le q^{j-1}\\sum_xw_x^2.\n \\label{base:weighted-variance}$$ In particular, for sets $S,T$ in the opposite spaces, $$\\left|e(S,T)-p_j|S||T|\\right|\n \\le q^{(j-1)/2}\\sqrt{|S||T|}.\n \\label{base:mixing}$$ If $S,T$ are nonempty and $e(S,T)/(|S||T|)=o(1/q)$, then $|S||T|\\le Cq^{j+1}$ for all sufficiently large $q$.",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "Q05",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": "1",
      "quantity": "incidence error",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Finite graphs and projective spaces; natural logarithms.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-base-incidence",

          "anchor": "stmt-base-selection-entropy",
          "exact_quote": "Let a random stream have law dominated by $C_0$ times the iid uniform flag-stream law, where $C_0$ is independent of $q$. From this stream, using arbitrary dependent auxiliary randomness, extract a tuple $F$ of deterministic length $\\ell=\\Theta(k)$. Its order may be either increasing stream order or reversed stream order with the endpoints interchanged. Then $$H(F)\\ge d\\sigma\\ell+\\eta\\ell\\log\\sigma-O(k).\n \\label{base:entropy-lower}$$ The implicit constant may depend on $C_0$ and the fixed comparison constants in $\\ell=\\Theta(k)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"
        }
      ],

    {
      "computational_model": null,
      "effectivity": "partially_effective",
      "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geom-description",
          "exact_quote": "Let $2\\leq j\\leq d$. Let $S,T$ be nonempty sets in opposite point systems of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(j,q)$, contained in decoder-known sets $U_S,U_T$. Suppose, for a fixed constant $C_b$, that $$\\label{geom:sparse-hypotheses}\n |S||T|\\geq q^{j+1}e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{e(S,T)}{|S||T|}\\leq\\frac{\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n 0\\leq b\\leq C_bK_*,\\quad\n 0<\\tau\\leq C_b\\sigma^{-100\\beta}.$$ Write $$d_S=\\log\\frac{|U_S|}{|S|},\\qquad\n d_T=\\log\\frac{|U_T|}{|T|},$$ and orient the systems so that $n=|S|\\leq |T|$. There is a public-table description, of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P),$$ which, except with probability $Ce^{-cq}$, produces a decoder-known set $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\\label{geom:description-output}\n |W|\\leq ne^{CP},\\qquad |W\\cap S|\\geq cn.$$ The probability bound holds conditional on any earlier data independent of the tables used by this invocation.",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",
      "id": "Q13",
      "kind": "bound",
      "prefactor": "C",
      "quantity": "description length",
      "regime": {
        "ambient": "Finite graphs and projective spaces; natural logarithms.",
        "hypotheses": [
          {
            "anchor": "stmt-geom-description",
            "exact_quote": "Let $2\\leq j\\leq d$. Let $S,T$ be nonempty sets in opposite point systems of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(j,q)$, contained in decoder-known sets $U_S,U_T$. Suppose, for a fixed constant $C_b$, that $$\\label{geom:sparse-hypotheses}\n |S||T|\\geq q^{j+1}e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{e(S,T)}{|S||T|}\\leq\\frac{\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n 0\\leq b\\leq C_bK_*,\\quad\n 0<\\tau\\leq C_b\\sigma^{-100\\beta}.$$ Write $$d_S=\\log\\frac{|U_S|}{|S|},\\qquad\n d_T=\\log\\frac{|U_T|}{|T|},$$ and orient the systems so that $n=|S|\\leq |T|$. There is a public-table description, of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P),$$ which, except with probability $Ce^{-cq}$, produces a decoder-known set $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\\label{geom:description-output}\n |W|\\leq ne^{CP},\\qquad |W\\cap S|\\geq cn.$$ The probability bound holds conditional on any earlier data independent of the tables used by this invocation.",

          {
            "anchor": "stmt-geom-description",
            "exact_quote": "Let $2\\leq j\\leq d$. Let $S,T$ be nonempty sets in opposite point systems of $\\mathop{\\mathrm{PG}}(j,q)$, contained in decoder-known sets $U_S,U_T$. Suppose, for a fixed constant $C_b$, that $$\\label{geom:sparse-hypotheses}\n |S||T|\\geq q^{j+1}e^{-b},\\qquad\n \\frac{e(S,T)}{|S||T|}\\leq\\frac{\\tau}{q},\\qquad\n 0\\leq b\\leq C_bK_*,\\quad\n 0<\\tau\\leq C_b\\sigma^{-100\\beta}.$$ Write $$d_S=\\log\\frac{|U_S|}{|S|},\\qquad\n d_T=\\log\\frac{|U_T|}{|T|},$$ and orient the systems so that $n=|S|\\leq |T|$. There is a public-table description, of length at most $$CqP(d_S+d_T+P),$$ which, except with probability $Ce^{-cq}$, produces a decoder-known set $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfying $$\\label{geom:description-output}\n |W|\\leq ne^{CP},\\qquad |W\\cap S|\\geq cn.$$ The probability bound holds conditional on any earlier data independent of the tables used by this invocation.",

        {
          "anchor": "stmt-geom-validation",
          "exact_quote": "Under <a href=\"#geom:sparse-hypotheses\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:sparse-hypotheses\">[geom:sparse-hypotheses]</a>, suppose a decoder-known $W\\subseteq U_S$ satisfies <a href=\"#geom:description-output\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"geom:description-output\">[geom:description-output]</a>. Further independent tables and a message of length $CqP(d_S+d_T+1)$ produce caps $U_T'\\subseteq U_T$ and $U_S'\\subseteq U_S$, with additional failure probability $Ce^{-cq}$, such that $$\\label{geom:validation-output}\n \\begin{aligned}\n |U_T'|&\\leq \\frac{Cq^{j+1}}{|S|},&\n |U_S'|&\\leq \\frac{Cq^{j+1}}{|T|},\\\\\n |U_T'\\cap T|&\\geq .99|T|,&\n |U_S'\\cap S|&\\geq .99|S|.\n \\end{aligned}$$ Each cap is its input domain intersected with an ambient test: a candidate passes if it is incident with fewer than $h/(5q)$ entries of the transmitted row of length $h$ in the opposite system.",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"
        }
      ],
      "fidelity": "PENDING",

      "kind": "bound",

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R01** [claim](#stmt-base-incidence) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-incidence): Incidence-matrix identity and centered operator norm -> Weighted variance and mixing
- **R02** [claim](#stmt-base-flag-graph) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-flag-graph): Ordered linear independence and negation of the edge rule -> Clique exclusion and consistency equivalence
- **R03** [claim](#stmt-base-selection-entropy) / [proof](#proof-stmt-base-selection-entropy): Atom bound, union bound and entropy -> Selected-tuple entropy surplus
- **R04** [claim](#stmt-geom-rich-lines) / [proof](#proof-stmt-geom-rich-lines): Interpolation, tangent-plane Hessian and coprime resultant -> Rich-line count
- **R05** [claim](#stmt-learn-short-row) / [proof](#proof-stmt-learn-implementation-2): Poisson batches, second/high moments and bounded proposal search -> Short-row support description
- **R06** [claim](#stmt-geom-description) / [proof](#proof-stmt-learn-implementation-3): Low-dimensional completion and bounded projection induction -> Sparse-pair description in every allowed fixed dimension
- **R07** [claim](#stmt-mark-good-indices) / [proof](#proof-stmt-mark-good-indices): Marking, law-determined exposure and rectangle charges -> Good representative and target laws
- **R08** [claim](#stmt-comp-step) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-tree-loss-2): High-rank two-row description or reciprocal chronological tree -> Replacement context and deterministic retained subtuple
- **R09** [claim](#stmt-intro-prime-construction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-comp-tree-loss-3): Bounded compression iteration and entropy contradiction -> Existence of prime-indexed avoiding graph
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-end-upper-bound) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-upper-bound): Clique-order induction, sampling and triangle deletion -> Upper Ramsey estimate
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-intro-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-end-upper-bound-2): Fixed-ratio prime transfer and logarithmic squeeze -> Sharp logarithmic exponent

Suppose that the stream law is dominated by a fixed constant times its original independent law and is supported on the rectangle-occupancy event of Lemma <a href=\"#base:rectangles\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"base:rectangles\">5</a>. Let a discrete context $\\mathsf C$ specify a domain for each slot, with $$H(\\mathsf C)\\le\\Lambda,\\qquad\n |\\text{domain of each slot}|\\le Cq^d e^\\Delta,\n \\qquad \\Lambda,\\Delta\\ge0.$$ Define, as in <a href=\"#mark:parameters\" data-reference-type=\"eqref\" data-reference=\"mark:parameters\">[mark:parameters]</a>, $$D=\\sigma^\\beta(1+\\Lambda/k+\\Delta\\sigma^{-\\eta}),\n \\qquad \\sigma^\\beta\\le D\\le\\sigma^{1-\\eta/2}.$$ There is an extraction of a consistent tuple $F'$ of deterministic length $\\ell'=\\Theta(k)$, still in one of the two stream orientations, and a new discrete context $\\mathsf C'$ for which $$\\label{comp:step-bounds}\n\\begin{split}\n H(\\mathsf C')&\\le\\Lambda'\n     \\le CqP\\log(w+2)(\\sigma+wP)+Cw\\sigma,\\\\\n |\\text{domain of each slot of }F'|\n     &\\le Cq^d e^{\\Delta'},\\qquad\n       \\Delta'\\le CK_*,\\\\\n &\\hspace{1em}1\\le w\\le C\\sigma^{1+\\eta/2}/D.\n\\end{split}$$ Here $w$ is a deterministic integer fixed during the preparation of the step. The new decoder uses only $\\mathsf C'$, fixed public parameters, and fixed public tables; it does not use $\\mathsf C$. The extraction is obtained by conditioning on an event of probability bounded below by a positive constant. Consequently its stream law still has constant domination and satisfies the same occupancy bound. All constants, including the retained proportion, are independent of $q$. They and the sufficiently-large-$q$ threshold are uniform over the input laws and contexts: they depend only on $d,\\eta,c_0$ and fixed numerical bounds for the domination constant and the domain prefactor, not on the realized history or on $\\Lambda,\\Delta$ within the stated admissible range.",

For every sufficiently large prime $q$, with $\\sigma=\\log q$, there is a graph $G$ such that $$|V(G)|=\\lfloor q^d\\sigma\\rfloor,\\qquad\n K_{d+1}\\not\\subseteq G,\\qquad\n \\alpha(G)<\\lfloor q\\sigma^{1+\\eta}\\rfloor.$$",
          "source_id": "PM-170.2"
        }

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **I1**: The ordered incident-flag graph is clique-free at order d+1, and independent position subsets are exactly consistent subtuples.
- **I2**: Repeated flag values remain distinct graph vertices.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **A1**: Claim an efficient optimal Ramsey graph generator. -> Description-length bounds give no practical search-time certificate.
- **A2**: Treat repeated flags as one graph vertex. -> Collapsing positions changes graph order and independent sets.
- **A3**: Claim a constant-factor sharp lower bound. -> The logarithmic exponent allows an epsilon loss.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. Finite ordered-flag graph kernel from Lemma 4; bounded implementation only. The main asymptotic graph existence and public-table searches are outside executable coverage.

```python
# PM-P3-HEADER: headers added deterministically from panel3 fields (missing in the professor code)
# INPUTS: JSON list of cases {q,d,flags}; q in {2,3}, 1<=d<=5, at most 6 incident flags; each endpoint is a nonzero integer vector of length d+1 with entries in [0,q). Positions remain distinct. Invalid cases return contract error. These implementation caps do not change the paper theorem.
# PRECONDITIONS: JSON list of cases {q,d,flags}; q in {2,3}, 1<=d<=5, at most 6 incident flags; each endpoint is a nonzero integer vector of length d+1 with entries in [0,q). Positions remain distinct. Invalid cases return contract error. These implementation caps do not change the paper theorem.
# OUTPUTS: JSON list of vertex counts, labeled edges, all consistent subset bitmasks, independence numbers and clique numbers; contract errors for rejected cases.
# TIME: ABSENT (not stated in panel3)
# SPACE: ABSENT (not stated in panel3)
# ARITHMETIC: ABSENT (not stated in panel3)
# FAILURE: Oracle rejects wrong edges and clique bounds.; Oracle rejects labeled edge sets on nonsymmetric ordered pairs.; Repeated stream boundary changes vertex count and independent subset masks.; Oracle rejects missing mask zero, including empty stream.
import json,sys,itertools
from flint import fmpz

def run(c):
    q,d,flags=c['q'],c['d'],c['flags']
    def incident(a,b):
        return sum((fmpz(x)*fmpz(y) for x,y in zip(a,b)),fmpz(0)) % q == 0
    if q not in (2,3) or not 1 <= d <= 5 or len(flags)>6:
        return {'error':'contract'}
    for a,b in flags:
        if len(a)!=d+1 or len(b)!=d+1 or not any(a) or not any(b) or any(type(x)!=int or not 0<=x<q for x in a+b) or not incident(a,b):
            return {'error':'contract'}
    n=len(flags)
    edges=[]
    for i in range(n):
        for j in range(i+1,n):
            if incident(flags[i][0],flags[j][1]) and not incident(flags[j][0],flags[i][1]):
                edges.append([i,j])
    consistent=[]
    omega=0
    for mask in range(1<<n):
        ids=[i for i in range(n) if mask>>i&1]
        if all(not incident(flags[i][0],flags[j][1]) or incident(flags[j][0],flags[i][1]) for i,j in itertools.combinations(ids,2)):
            consistent.append(mask)
        if all([i,j] in edges for i,j in itertools.combinations(ids,2)):
            omega=max(omega,len(ids))
    return {'n':n,'edges':edges,'consistent':consistent,'alpha':max(map(int.bit_count,consistent)),'omega':omega}
print(json.dumps([run(c) for c in json.load(sys.stdin)],sort_keys=True,separators=(',',':')))
```

| tools | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | A specialized checker can compare graph edges against an ordered flag stream and compare independent subsets against consistency. This contract applies only to the represented flag graph. |
| algorithms | True | moderate | implementable | inferred | The explicitly stated finite edge predicate is implementable. The public-table decoder is a mathematical finite-search scheme with a message bound; no efficient implementation of the main existential Ramsey construction follows. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The ordered stream and chronological tree specify mathematical organization, but a data-structure operation contract or complexity result is ABSENT. Preserving positions is a representation constraint, not a new data-structure result. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | An accelerator design, hardware computational model, and throughput or memory measurements are ABSENT; exact projective incidence alone supplies no hardware contract. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L01** (approximation): The sharp logarithmic exponent does not establish a positive constant-factor lower bound at the matching logarithmic power. Blocked use: ['Claiming a matching Theta estimate or a leading asymptotic coefficient.']
- **L02** (hypothesis): The main theorem fixes the clique parameter and takes the independent-set parameter sufficiently large; uniform growing-clique or small-input guarantees are not supplied. Blocked use: ['Using the main lower bound for growing s, small t, or clique parameters outside its stated range.']
- **L03** (nonconstructive): The prime-indexed existence theorem does not guarantee that an arbitrary sampled stream has the asserted independence number. An explicit finite threshold for sufficiently large prime parameters is ABSENT. Blocked use: ['Certifying independence-number bounds of a sampled graph without checking it.', 'Inventing a numeric applicability threshold.']
- **L04** (counterexample_scope): The finite clique-free and consistency guarantees apply to the specified ordered incident-flag edge rule. They do not certify graphs produced by arbitrary adjacency rules. Blocked use: ['General graph clique-freeness from projective endpoints alone.']
- **L05** (edge_case): Repeated flag values are permitted; positions must remain distinct graph vertices. Blocked use: ['Deduplicating flags or collapsing equal endpoints into one vertex.']
- **L06** (nonconstructive): The encoding results bound mathematical message length and do not give practical search-time guarantees. Blocked use: ['Polynomial-time or production runtime/memory guarantees for public-table searches.']
- **L07** (hypothesis): Selected-tuple entropy permits arbitrary selection bias only with a constant stream-domination factor and deterministic tuple length at the specified scale. Blocked use: ['Treating a selected tuple as iid.', 'Using the entropy lower bound with unrestricted domination or random length.']
- **L08** (hypothesis): Sparse-pair descriptions require nonempty opposite projective supports inside decoder-known containing domains and the displayed sparse-product hypotheses. Blocked use: ['Generic sparse-data compression without the projective incidence model or without checking every hypothesis.']
- **L09** (hypothesis): Produced validation-row law comparisons hold at readiness and on production, without conditioning on later events. Blocked use: ['Reusing the row-law domination after later success conditioning.', 'Applying validation-row domination to the accepted description row.']
- **L10** (approximation): The accepted proposal row is not asserted to have an unbiased true-law distribution; later validation requires a fresh table. Blocked use: ['Statistical unbiasedness or reuse of the accepted description table as fresh validation randomness.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The rich-line argument keeps surface degrees below the prime field characteristic; extending it to unrelated fields or degrees requires additional proof. Blocked use: ['Transferring the polynomial-method bound to small-characteristic extension fields without checking its hypotheses.']
- **L12** (hypothesis): The larger ambient exceptional set in the high-moment argument is not available for support-capture losses. Blocked use: ['Discarding the ambient exceptional set from the hidden support.']
- **L13** (approximation): Auxiliary-support sparsity is an average guarantee before support-dependent selection, not a property of every realized support pair. Blocked use: ['Replacing the expected incidence bound by a universal deterministic sparse-pair guarantee.']
- **L14** (hypothesis): Each replacement decoder must reconstruct from its new context and fixed public tables, without old history or hidden conditional laws. Blocked use: ['Accumulating all previous contexts while claiming the replacement entropy budget.']

<a id="pm-formal"></a>
### Formal statement (Lean 4)

Corpus status: formalized in the source corpus (toolchain `leanprover/lean4:v4.34.1`, Mathlib `d13f23b723b8a846827a245b89c10fc7d3f11612`). Not re-checked here; the statements give the exact quantifiers and hypotheses, not a proof.

Scope note of family 170 (`lean/docs/170.md`, verbatim):

> The following describes the scope of the Lean formalization related to the following accompanying paper(s):
>
> - [The sharp logarithmic exponent of $r(5,t)$](../../preprints/The-Sharp-Logarithmic-Exponent-of-r-5-t-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
> - [Sharp Logarithmic Exponents for Fixed Off-Diagonal Ramsey Numbers](../../preprints/Sharp-Logarithmic-Exponents-for-Fixed-Off-Diagonal-Ramsey-Numbers-September-24-2026/paper.pdf)
>
> ## Scope
>
> The formalization determines the sharp logarithmic exponent of the off-diagonal Ramsey number $r(5,t)$. It proves
> $r(5,t)=t^4/(\log t)^{3+o(1)}$ as $t\to\infty$.
> For every $\varepsilon>0$, the lower bound $t^4/(\log t)^{3+\varepsilon}$ holds eventually, while the upper bound is $Ct^4/(\log t)^3$ for an absolute $C>0$. The corresponding logarithmic exponent converges to three along all natural values of $t$.
>
> The formalization determines the sharp logarithmic exponent of the off-diagonal Ramsey number for every fixed integer $s\ge6$. It proves
> $r(s,t)=t^{s-1}/(\log t)^{s-2+o(1)}$ as $t\to\infty$.
> More explicitly, for each $\varepsilon>0$ the lower bound $t^{s-1}/(\log t)^{s-2+\varepsilon}$ holds eventually, while the upper bound is $C_s t^{s-1}/(\log t)^{s-2}$. The logarithmic exponent limit is taken along all natural values of $t$.

| declaration | kind |
|---|---|
| `RamseyProperty` | def |
| `ramsey` | def |
| `MainBounds` | def |
| `MainLimit` | def |
| `main` | theorem |

Comparator source `SharpLogRamsey.lean` (1209 bytes, sha256 `a47b22011f304a35ccc9dac49ff7342c7f1312caa1860b7ca759ab4aeb6dda47`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib

namespace OAI

namespace SharpLogRamsey

/-- Every graph has an s-clique or an independent set of size t. -/
def RamseyProperty (s t N : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∀ G : SimpleGraph (Fin N),
    (∃ A : Finset (Fin N), G.IsNClique s A) ∨
    (∃ B : Finset (Fin N), Gᶜ.IsNClique t B)

noncomputable def ramsey (s t : ℕ) : ℕ := sInf {N | RamseyProperty s t N}

/-- The upper-bound constant is chosen independently of epsilon. -/
def MainBounds (s : ℕ) : Prop :=
  ∃ C : ℝ, 0 < C ∧ ∀ ε : ℝ, 0 < ε →
    ∃ t₀ : ℕ, ∀ t : ℕ, t₀ ≤ t →
      (t : ℝ) ^ (s - 1) / Real.rpow (Real.log (t : ℝ)) ((s - 2 : ℕ) + ε)
        ≤ (ramsey s t : ℝ) ∧
      (ramsey s t : ℝ) ≤ C * (t : ℝ) ^ (s - 1) /
        (Real.log (t : ℝ)) ^ (s - 2)

/-- The logarithmic exponent converges along all natural orders. -/
def MainLimit (s : ℕ) : Prop :=
  Filter.Tendsto
    (fun t : ℕ =>
      (((s - 1 : ℕ) : ℝ) * Real.log (t : ℝ) - Real.log (ramsey s t : ℝ)) /
        Real.log (Real.log (t : ℝ)))
    Filter.atTop (nhds ((s - 2 : ℕ) : ℝ))

theorem main (s : ℕ) (hs : 6 ≤ s) : MainBounds s ∧ MainLimit s := by
  sorry

end SharpLogRamsey

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


For every fixed integer <i>s</i> ≥ 6, we determine the sharp logarithmic exponent of the off-diagonal Ramsey number:

$`\displaystyle r(s,t)=\frac{t^{s-1}}{(\log t)^{s-2+o(1)}} \qquad (t\longrightarrow\infty).`$
